\subsection{Translation-Gated Structural-Order Boundary for $B^*_{Q6}$}\label{sec:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-cross-layer}
\origin{ai}

\paragraph{Finite object.}
This chapter records a finite cross-layer test surface for the claim that a synonymous residual coordinate in $B^*_{Q6}$ may enter a structural-order analysis only through a prior translation readout gate. The object is not a general biological law and not a folding theorem. It is a CDS-indexed table whose rows are encoded proteins and whose columns are nine amino-acid-quotient residual coordinates,
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal Q_{6}^{*} = \{&K_{AAA},\ Arg_{AGR},\ Ile_{AUA},\ Leu_{CUN\,vs\,UUR},\ Leu_{UUA\,vs\,UUG},\\
&Ser_{UCR\,vs\,AGY},\ Ser_{UCA\,vs\,UCG},\ Thr_{ACR\,vs\,ACY},\ f3_{stress}\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Each coordinate is paired with organism-matched codon-count information, a controlled residual design, and a readout column. The present finite run attaches AlphaFold mean pLDDT as the only structure-adjacent readout. It is interpreted strictly as predicted structure confidence on the $0$ to $100$ scale, not as an experimental folding measurement and not as molecular function.

\paragraph{Gate condition.}
The admissible route is a two-stage chain. A coordinate $q_i$ must first survive a translation-layer test against a readout $T_j$ after conditioning on the declared baseline variables. Only then may a structure-adjacent readout $SO$ be tested for remaining association, and that second test must condition on the translation readout and protein-abundance baseline when those columns are present. In symbols, the proposed route is
$$
\begin{aligned}
q_i \longrightarrow T_j \longrightarrow SO,
\end{aligned}
$$
not a direct promotion from codon geometry to structure. If no $q_i$ survives the translation-readout stage, all entries in the structural-order table are null by construction. In the powered finite row currently available, the recorded computation directly evaluates the predicted-confidence association after the sequence controls; it therefore supports only a bounded cross-sectional association with predicted confidence and does not by itself establish the full mediated chain through measured translation.

\paragraph{Controls and residual design.}
The control matrix $Z$ contains an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, twenty amino-acid composition proportions computed from real CDS codon counts, GC3 from real CDS codon counts, and an $M$-density baseline defined as the fraction of sense codons on the union support of the nine projected $B^*_{Q6}$ coordinate vectors. The amino-acid composition columns are the proportions for $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$. Thus a positive residual entry cannot be read as an amino-acid-composition effect alone.

\paragraph{Rank and row diagnostics.}
The finite computation identified full coordinate rank after the declared controls in both organisms. In \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, the control column count was $24$, the rank of $Z$ was $23$, the residualized coordinate rank was $9$, and the residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ were $3713$. The explained energy was $4178.935925251874$, while the residual readout energy was $136607.52873622958$. In \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, the same $24$ control columns gave rank$(Z)=23$, rank$(\widetilde X)=9$, and residual degrees of freedom $5781$. The explained energy was $44421.14342043593$, and the residual readout energy was $620506.1490869389$. The degeneracy threshold used for the diagnostic was $R^2=0.999999$, and both organisms were marked rank-sufficient for the nine-coordinate test.

\paragraph{Matched structural-order rows.}
The predicted-confidence readout was joined to codon rows at high finite coverage. For \emph{E. coli} K12 MG1655, $3739$ codon-joined rows were available, $3736$ matched proteins were used, $3736$ proteins had the structural-order readout, and the join hit rate was $0.999198$. For \emph{S. cerevisiae}, $5815$ codon-joined rows were available, $5804$ matched proteins were used, $5804$ proteins had the structural-order readout, and the join hit rate was $0.998108$. These numbers make the finite table non-vacuous, but they do not change the layer of the readout: the column is predicted structure confidence, not a direct measurement of folded state, function, phenotype, localization, pathway activity, or folding kinetics.

\paragraph{Observed finite association.}
Using the positive partial-$R^2$ threshold $10^{-12}$, all nine residual coordinates were recorded as surviving the predicted-confidence association test in each organism. The structural-order residual score was
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{SO}(\textit{E. coli}\ \mathrm{K12\ MG1655}) &= 0.03059081709413562,\\
R^2_{SO}(\textit{S. cerevisiae}) &= 0.0715885627979685.
\end{aligned}
$$
The \emph{E. coli} signal is therefore a modest finite association, and the yeast signal is a stronger finite association in the same bounded sense. The result is cross-sectional and associational. It says that the nine-coordinate residual surface has detectable predictive-confidence association after length, amino-acid composition, GC3, and $M$-density controls in these two organism-specific tables.

\paragraph{Local null boundary.}
The finite result must be read with its restrictions intact. AlphaFold mean pLDDT is predicted confidence, not an experimental structural-order assay. tAI, stAI, ribosome occupancy, translation efficiency, and protein abundance are not folded-structure measurements. A codon residual coordinate, an $M$-density contrast, or an internal coordinate geometry cannot by itself imply translation realization, folding, physical admissibility, molecular function, organismal phenotype, mechanism, or universality. A stronger structural-order claim would require a separate structure-level contact and a same-coordinate test performed only after the translation readout gate has survived under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, $M$-density, simpler-baseline, mRNA, and protein-abundance controls as appropriate. Without that attached contact and gated statistic, the present chapter reports only the finite predicted-confidence association described above.

\origin{ai}

\subsection{Direct protein-omics survival with an explicit translation boundary}
\label{sec:q6-direct-protein-omics-survival-with-translation-boundary}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
The record is a finite CDS-by-organism table whose rows are coding sequences with attached organism labels, amino-acid composition, GC3, CDS length, methionine density, B$^{*}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates, and measured protein abundance. The residual coordinate list used in the powered protein-omics run has nine entries:
$K\_AAA$, $Arg\_AGR$, $Ile\_AUA$, $Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR$, $Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG$, $Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY$, $Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG$, $Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY$, and $f3\_stress$. These coordinates are read after the amino-acid quotient, so the test is not a test of amino-acid composition alone. It asks whether synonymous residual shape has remaining predictive content for a protein-omics readout after the declared baseline variables have been removed.

\paragraph{Protein-omics contact actually computed.}
The measured downstream column is $\log_{10}(\mathrm{abundance\_ppm})$ from PAXdb protein abundance joined to real CDS codon counts. Three organism-specific finite matrices were used. For \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, $3739$ proteins were joined with hit rate $0.9978649586335735$; for \emph{Mycobacterium smegmatis} str. MC2 155, $4129$ proteins were joined with hit rate $1.0$; for \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, $5815$ proteins were joined with hit rate $0.9156038419146592$. In each organism the residualized B$^{*}_{Q6}$ design retained rank $9$ after projecting away the control space. The control matrix had $24$ columns, rank $23$, and the degeneracy threshold for rejecting vacuous fits was $0.999999$.

\paragraph{Controlled survival statistic.}
Let $X_Q$ denote the nine-column B$^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinate matrix, let $Z$ denote the baseline control matrix, and let $Y_P$ denote the protein-abundance readout. The direct protein-omics score is the partial predictive residue of $X_Q$ for $Y_P$ after residualizing against $Z$:
$$
\begin{aligned}
S^{QP} &= R^2\bigl((I-\Pi_Z)Y_P \;\big|\; (I-\Pi_Z)X_Q\bigr),\\
Z &= \{\text{amino-acid composition},\text{ organism},GC3,\text{ length},M\text{-density}\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
The computed organism-level values were positive in the finite data: $R^2_{QP}=0.10767526677985896$ for \emph{E. coli} K12 MG1655 with residual degrees of freedom $3716$, $R^2_{QP}=0.03209009170649934$ for \emph{M. smegmatis} with residual degrees of freedom $4106$, and $R^2_{QP}=0.29837624994140827$ for \emph{S. cerevisiae} with residual degrees of freedom $5792$. All nine residual coordinates survived the positive threshold $10^{-12}$ in each organism-specific fit. The corresponding residual response energies were $4020.333671802061$, $5729.194538214237$, and $2958.1865890925337$, while the control-explained energies were $432.89050065533684$, $183.85037813566998$, and $882.6526210803958$ respectively.

\paragraph{Translation boundary.}
This positive $S^{QP}$ surface is not yet a translation mechanism. The direct protein-omics run is a cross-sectional abundance association and explicitly lacks perturbation, rescue, ribosome-profiling mediation, and matched transcript-control mediation. A separate translation column $T_j$ must be attached before the record can distinguish direct protein-omics prediction from a translation-mediated chain. The required next matrix has the same CDS rows and must contain measured or explicitly bounded translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein abundance. Only then can one evaluate whether $q_i \to T_j$ survives and whether conditioning on $T_j$ removes, reduces, or leaves the $q_i \to P_k$ signal:
$$
\begin{aligned}
S^{QT}_{ij} &> 0,\\
S^{QP}_{ik} &> 0,\\
M^{QTP}_{ijk} &= \text{attenuation of } q_i\to P_k \text{ after conditioning on } T_j.
\end{aligned}
$$
Until that joined matrix exists, the present result should be read only as DNA-to-protein-omics predictive irreducibility under the stated controls.

\paragraph{Modeled translation readout already separated.}
A distinct bounded translation-readout computation used modeled tAI weights from GtRNAdb tRNA gene-copy data and dos Reis wobble weights. It covered $61$ sense codons in each organism, with zero raw-weight fallback count $0$ for \emph{E. coli} K12, human, and budding yeast. The local tRNA payload contained all $20$ standard amino-acid families in each case: \emph{E. coli} had $89$ FASTA headers, $87$ matched headers, and $2$ unmatched headers; human had $432$ headers, all matched; budding yeast had $275$ headers, all matched. The modeled tAI bounded-coupling matrix had $81$ entries above threshold $0.01$, with maximum absolute entry $3801961.031607896$. This establishes a bounded modeled-contact signal, not a powered measured translation-survival claim: the organism count was $3$, and the record itself refuses powered survival without at least $40$ matched species.

\paragraph{Local no-promotion rule.}
The finite evidence supports a direct association between B$^{*}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates and measured protein abundance after strong baseline residualization. It does not establish ribosome dynamics, translation rate, folded structure, localization, post-translational state, biochemical function, physical admissibility, phenotype, causal mechanism, or a universal biological law. Each of those claims requires its own layer-matched contact and its own controlled statistic. In particular, a positive $S^{QP}$ entry must not be reported as translation-mediated protein realization unless a measured $T_j$ column is present and the fitted mediation matrix supports $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$ rather than only $q_i \to P_k$.

\paragraph{Status.}
The direct protein-omics part is finite-row positive and rank-admissible for the three organism-specific protein-abundance tables described above. The full conjecture remains data-incomplete because the mandatory translation boundary has not been closed by same-row measured translation readouts and matched mRNA controls in the protein-omics mediation matrix. The correct present verdict is therefore: direct protein-omics survival observed under baseline controls; translation-mediated interpretation still withheld.

\origin{ai}

\subsection{Translation-mediated protein-omics boundary for the $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinates}
\label{subsec:q6-translation-mediated-protein-function-boundary-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
This record concerns a finite matrix of cells $(q_i,T_j,P_k,F_l)$.  The first coordinate $q_i$ is one of the amino-acid-quotient residual coordinates of the $B^{*}_{Q6}$ coding-sequence basis; it is not an amino-acid composition variable and is interpreted only after amino-acid composition, GC3, coding-sequence length, organism, and the $M$-density baseline have been controlled.  The coordinate list used by the finite run contains nine rows,
$$
\begin{aligned}
R_{Q6}=\{&K\_AAA,\ Arg\_AGR,\ Ile\_AUA,\ Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR,\ Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG,\\
&Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY,\ Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG,\ Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY,\ f3\_stress\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
The symbol $T_j$ denotes a measured or explicitly bounded translation readout such as ribosome profiling, translation efficiency, or a codon-adaptation weight kept at translation scope.  The symbol $P_k$ denotes a protein-omics intermediary such as matched protein abundance.  The symbol $F_l$ is present only as a closed door: it is not populated by this record unless a separate functional or phenotypic assay is attached later.  Thus the admissible direction of testing is $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$ before any statement involving $F_l$ is even considered.

\paragraph{Observed finite rows.}
The finite computation named \texttt{ebba7436a62a} used real coding-sequence codon counts and measured protein abundance for two organisms.  In \emph{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655, the joined table retained $3737$ genes, with $285$ essential and $3452$ nonessential labels, so the essential fraction was $0.07626438319507627$.  The abundance join reported $3739$ records, and the unique essentiality-label count was $3737$.  In \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, the retained table contained $5511$ genes, with $1168$ essential and $4343$ nonessential labels, giving essential fraction $0.2119397568499365$; the abundance join reported $5815$ records and $5511$ unique essentiality labels.  Both organism rows had both essentiality classes present, so the binary readback was nonconstant before residualization.

\paragraph{Controls and rank.}
The control design used an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, the twenty amino-acid composition proportions $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$, GC3, the $M$-density baseline on the union support of the nine $B^{*}_{Q6}$ vectors, and $\log_{10}(\mathrm{abundance\_ppm})$.  Hence the recorded control-column count was $25$.  For both organisms the residualized $q$-design had rank $9$, matching the nine-coordinate list, while the control matrix had rank $24$.  The residual degrees of freedom after the control block were $3713$ for \emph{E. coli} and $5487$ for yeast.  The corresponding residual response energies were $232.13814987247684$ and $826.488384003322$.  The explained energies attributed to the residual $B^{*}_{Q6}$ block after the controls were $2.243840654793728$ in \emph{E. coli} and $2.741892921952373$ in yeast.  Both rows were marked rank-powered rather than saturated, using degeneracy threshold $0.999999$.

\paragraph{Essentiality readback.}
The attached downstream readout in this finite run was a lab knockout essentiality label joined through protein identifiers to real CDS codon counts and measured protein abundance.  It is a phenotype-adjacent binary laboratory readout, not a molecular translation measurement and not a functional assay.  After amino-acid composition and measured protein-abundance controls, all nine residual coordinates had positive entries above the declared positive partial-$R^2$ threshold $10^{-12}$ for this essentiality readback.  The finite summary reports
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{FE}(\emph{E. coli}) &= 0.00966597112980509,\\
R^2_{FE}(\emph{S. cerevisiae}) &= 0.003317521425614316.
\end{aligned}
$$
These numbers are small, finite, and useful only as bounded associations under the stated residualization.  They do not show synonymous-edit causality, ribosome mechanism, pathway mechanism, expression causality, or organism-general law.  The binary model was an ordinary least-squares linear-probability residualization, so the result should be read as a controlled association surface rather than as a mechanistic probability model.

\paragraph{Mediation gate.}
The scientific claim of this packet is conditional.  A residual coordinate may approach the protein-omics boundary only if the same $q_i$ first survives against an attached translation readout $T_j$, and only if the later $q_i \to P_k$ association is reduced or absorbed when $T_j$ is included.  In finite-row terms, the desired discriminant separates four outcomes:
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{case}\ 0 &: \text{no controlled survival},\\
\mathrm{case}\ T &: \text{translation-only survival},\\
\mathrm{case}\ P &: \text{protein-omics survival not mediated by measured translation},\\
\mathrm{case}\ T\!P &: \text{protein-omics survival mediated by the translation readout}.
\end{aligned}
$$
The current essentiality computation does not by itself assign a translation-mediated protein-function case.  It supplies a rank-powered residual association after protein-abundance control and establishes that the relevant matrices are not rank-degenerate for the two tested organisms.  A separate same-CDS translation row, such as measured translation efficiency from matched ribosome profiling and mRNA abundance, is still required before the protein-omics mediation statement can be promoted from boundary language to a positive mediated readback.

\paragraph{No-promotion boundary.}
The $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinates are coordinate memory over coding sequences, not observations of ribosome motion.  Modeled tAI or stAI weights may be used as bounded translation-layer annotations, but they are not ribosome profiling, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, or universality evidence.  Matched mRNA abundance is a control for translation-efficiency residualization, not a translation realization by itself.  Matched proteomics abundance is a downstream protein-omics contact, not a folding assay or a functional assay.  Therefore no translation mechanism, folding claim, physical-admissibility claim, function-realization claim, phenotype claim, causal mechanism, or global biological rule follows from this chapter unless an additional layer-matched contact supplies that readout and the corresponding controlled statistic is computed.

\paragraph{Local verdict.}
The finite rows support a bounded cross-layer boundary: the nine $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinates remain testable after amino-acid quotienting and the stated controls, and the two organism rows are rank-powered for the essentiality association surface.  The observed $R^2_{FE}$ values, $0.00966597112980509$ and $0.003317521425614316$, are recorded as controlled residual associations only.  The chapter therefore admits the protein-function boundary as a guarded research object, but it keeps function null until a separate functional or phenotypic contact is attached after a surviving translation step and a demonstrated $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$ mediation pattern.

\origin{ai}

\subsection{Rank-powered modeled translation survival for the Q6 residual surface}
\label{subsec:q6-rank-powered-translation-survival-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite surface.}
This record concerns a finite organism-by-CDS readout surface in which the $B^{*}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates are compared with organism-scoped translation-readout weights after amino-acid quotient residualization. The coordinate side is not the raw codon table. Each coordinate $q_i$ is admitted only through its amino-acid-quotient residual form $q_i-P_{\tau}q_i$, so that a codon split cannot survive merely because it names a different amino acid class or a larger coding-sequence composition block. The readout side is likewise restricted: each modeled translation column $T_j$ is evaluated after controls for amino-acid composition, organism identity, GC3, CDS length, $M$-density, and simpler codon-usage baselines. The finite readout asks whether any residual coordinate keeps positive predictive mass for a translation-weight coordinate once those controls have already absorbed the obvious compositional explanations.

\paragraph{Controlled statistic.}
The powered run uses $40$ organisms and $9$ residual coordinates. The coordinate list is $K\_AAA$, $Arg\_AGR$, $Ile\_AUA$, $Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR$, $Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG$, $Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY$, $Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG$, $Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY$, and $f3\_stress$. The modeled translation readout has the same $9$ named columns, obtained from per-codon tAI weights computed from GtRNAdb anticodon copy counts and dos Reis wobble weights, then projected onto the same synonymous-residual coordinate system. The control design contains $24$ columns with $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$, leaving $17$ residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ is removed. The residualized coordinate block has $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$, so the powered panel is not the small-sample all-fit boundary.

$$
\begin{aligned}
  n_{\mathrm{org}} &= 40,\\
  \#Q &= 9,\\
  \#T &= 9,\\
  \operatorname{rank}(Z) &= 23,\\
  \operatorname{df}_{\mathrm{resid}} &= 17,\\
  \operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X) &= 9.
\end{aligned}
$$

On this finite surface the computed $S^{QT}$ matrix has shape $9\times 9$ and total controlled fit $R^2_{QT}=0.9003849000036579$. The positivity threshold for a partial-$R^2$ survival entry is $10^{-12}$, while the declared saturation boundary rejects fits at or above $0.999999$ as degenerate. Since $0.9003849000036579<0.999999$, this run is rank-admissible as a modeled tAI or stAI-weight readout rather than an all-ones projection artifact.

$$
\begin{aligned}
  R^2_{QT} &= 0.9003849000036579,\\
  \theta_{+} &= 10^{-12},\\
  \theta_{\mathrm{sat}} &= 0.999999,\\
  R^2_{QT} &< \theta_{\mathrm{sat}}.
\end{aligned}
$$

\paragraph{Three-organism refusal boundary.}
The earlier finite check used only $3$ organisms: \emph{Homo sapiens}, \emph{Escherichia coli} K12, and \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}. It computed modeled tAI weights over the $61$ sense codons and found all $81$ modeled coupling entries above the bounded-contact threshold $0.01$, with maximum absolute entry $3801961.031607896$. Those numbers show that the coding and tRNA-copy tables can be made to couple in a finite modeled calculation, but they do not by themselves constitute powered survival. With $n=3$, the controlled surface is a rank-saturation warning: an all-$1.0$ survival matrix in that setting is a null boundary for the population-level claim, not a biological discovery.

\paragraph{Readback boundary.}
The supported statement is therefore narrow. In the $40$-organism panel, $B^{*}_{Q6}$ has a rank-powered modeled translation-weight survival surface after amino-acid quotient residualization and declared controls, with an admissible $9\times 9$ $S^{QT}$ matrix and non-saturated total fit. This can be read as bounded survival against modeled codon-adaptation weights derived from tRNA gene-copy and wobble-weight tables. It is not a measurement of ribosome occupancy, elongation speed, translation efficiency from ribosome profiling, protein abundance, folding, structural order, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, mechanism, or a universal biological law. Any such stronger statement would require a separate layer-matched contact and a new controlled statistic on matched rows.

\paragraph{Break condition.}
The claim breaks if every controlled entry satisfies $S^{QT}_{ij}\leq 0$, if the apparent survival is absorbed by amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, $M$-density, or simpler codon-usage baselines, or if the fit enters the saturation regime. The $n=3$ modeled-tAI calculation is already assigned to that refusal role for powered inference. The $n=40$ calculation escapes the saturation threshold for the modeled tAI readout, but it remains bounded to modeled translation weights until measured ribosome, TE, or protein-output rows are joined and analyzed under the same control discipline.

\subsection{q6.rank-powered.translation-conditioned.protein-mediation.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested on a rank-powered matched panel containing codon usage, tAI or stAI translation readouts, ribosome-profiling translation efficiency, matched mRNA abundance, and matched protein abundance per CDS

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched organism panel, at least one B*\_Q6 residual coordinate has positive translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT and positive protein-abundance survival S\textasciicircum{}QP, and the corresponding direct q\_i -> P\_k signal is absorbed by a measured translation readout T\_j after mRNA and amino-acid-composition controls; otherwise B*\_Q6 remains bounded to translation readout or direct protein-omics prediction without mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matrix surface over B*\_Q6 residual coordinates q\_i, matched translation readouts T\_j, matched protein-abundance readouts P\_k, and transcript controls per CDS across a rank-powered organism panel. Distinctions: translation-surviving versus translation-null residual coordinate; protein-omics-surviving versus protein-omics-null residual coordinate; mediated q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k signal versus direct or confounded q\_i -> P\_k signal. Readback: per-entry four-case readback A/B/C/D after residualizing Q, T, and P against amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and matched mRNA controls. Internal structure: coordinate; rank; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.m-only-local-optimum-insufficiency}. If M-only is the true BEDC variable for DNA-to-protein, then for every organism and every readout in \{tAI, ribosome occupancy, protein abundance\}, adding any element of B*\_Q6 to an M-density baseline (with amino-acid composition controlled) does not increase the BEDC survival score. Equivalently, S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) <= 0 for every Q in B*\_Q6.

Support condition: Codon-usage tables for at least 3 organisms with tRNA-Leu, tRNA-Arg, tRNA-Ile, tRNA-Ser, tRNA-Thr copy-number data (e.g. Kazusa + GtRNAdb), plus a readout source (stAI or ribosome-profiling-derived efficiency).

Break condition: Some Q in B*\_Q6 has S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) > 0 in at least one organism on at least one readout; or CUN/UUR Leu log-ratio shows sign reversal between organisms after amino-acid composition control while Delta\_M does not.

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.system-phenotype-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested against matched translation readouts before any downstream system phenotype readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} If a rank-powered matched panel supplies B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, mRNA controls, and phenotype labels on the same CDS or organism surface, then any admissible Q6-to-phenotype frontier claim must decompose through translation first: q\_i may enter the system\_phenotype layer only as q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k, with the direct phenotype association rejected as a boundary artifact whenever the controlled translation leg is absent, rank-degenerate, or erased by baseline ablation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS-by-organism matrix carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, and system phenotype labels on the same readout surface. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving codon residual q\_i is separated from amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls; translation readout T\_j is explicitly measured or externally supplied before any phenotype readout is tested; phenotype association is admitted only after conditioning on the mandatory translation readout. Readback: per-entry gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k with break\_condition, AA-composition control, simpler-baseline ablation, and refusal of phenotype interpretation when the translation leg is absent or rank-degenerate. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.physical-admissibility-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream physical-admissibility readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a physical-admissibility boundary only through a decomposition that first tests translation-readout survival and then asks whether any downstream physical-admissibility association remains or is attenuated after conditioning on the translation readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, translation readout surfaces, optional protein-omics readbacks, and a future physical-admissibility readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i is amino-acid-quotient residualized before any readout test; T\_j is a measured or modeled translation readout, not a protein mechanism by itself; A physical-admissibility signal may be interpreted only after q\_i -> T\_j survival or an explicit conditioned boundary test. Readback: conditioned cross-layer table separating q\_i -> T\_j survival, q\_i -> physical-admissibility residual association, and translation-conditioned attenuation. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.post-translational-state-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream post-translational protein-state readout such as PTM, localization, or stability is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a post-translational protein-state boundary only through the gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k: first translation-readout survival must be established under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls, and only then may a matched PTM, localization, or stability readout be tested for mediation rather than direct codon-topology association.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by translation-readout by post-translational protein-state readout surface over matched CDS panels. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient before any protein-state interpretation; translation readout T\_j is measured or modeled separately from protein-state readout P\_k; conditioning on T\_j must reduce any direct q\_i to P\_k association before a translation-mediated protein-state claim is allowed. Readback: per-entry discriminant for q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k with null, translation-only, direct protein-state, and mediated cases reported separately. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\paragraph{Mismatch ledger.}

Delta: The tAI or stAI contact supplies an external organism-level codon-adaptation weight readback for the residual-basis packet, while BEDC residual coordinates and S\_Q notation remain internal coordinate memory. This contact does not observe ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or a global biological law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the packet at bounded readout scope: a separate reality contact is required before any ribosome, protein-output, mechanism, structure, physical-admissibility, function, or universal-law claim; codon/window geometry cannot supply that contact.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-stability-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream protein-stability or degradation-rate readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-stability boundary only through a rank-powered matched panel where q\_i first survives a translation readout T\_j under controls, and any downstream stability or degradation readout is reported as translation-conditioned mediation rather than direct codon-topology mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite organism-by-CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, matched translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein-stability readout surface where available. Distinctions: each B*\_Q6 coordinate is first amino-acid-quotient residualized; translation readout T\_j must survive controls before any protein-stability readout is interpreted; protein-stability signal is reported only as translation-conditioned residual association, not folding mechanism or phenotype. Readback: per-entry gated matrix q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-stability readout with AA-composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory matched translation readouts before any downstream protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as relevant to protein localization only through a gated cross-layer decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k: first test translation-readout survival with amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls, then attach a matched localization readout before interpreting any q\_i-to-localization relation. With the current contact set, the localization leg remains a needs-data boundary rather than a biological claim.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite CDS-indexed surface whose coordinates are B*\_Q6 synonymous residuals, matched translation readouts, mRNA controls, and a downstream protein-localization readout slot. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives the amino-acid quotient before any higher-layer readout is tested; T\_j translation readout is measured or modeled separately from the downstream protein-localization readout; localization interpretation is refused unless q\_i first survives or is conditioned through a translation-readout boundary. Readback: read back only the gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> localization\_k, with a null boundary when matched localization contacts are absent. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

This contact does not observe ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or a global biological law.

\subsection{q6.n3-rank-degenerate.translation-survival-refusal.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates tested against modeled tAI translation readouts across the current three-organism panel under amino-acid composition, GC3, length, and M-density controls

\paragraph{Current claim.} If the controlled B*\_Q6 to modeled-tAI survival matrix is computed on only the current three organisms, the resulting all-1.0 fit must be classified as rank saturation rather than biological survival; the admissible next step is a rank-powered translation-readout panel, not downstream interpretation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite 3-organism B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by modeled-tAI readout surface with control-residualized row space. Distinctions: rank-degenerate all-1.0 survival output is separated from powered translation-readout survival; B*\_Q6 residual coordinates remain translation-readout candidates rather than accepted survival coordinates at n=3. Readback: reject any S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} pass call when organism count and residual degrees of freedom make the controlled projection saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\paragraph{Mismatch ledger.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion.scope-review}. Status: aligned. Delta: The tAI or stAI contact supplies an external organism-level codon-adaptation weight readback for the residual-basis packet, while BEDC residual coordinates and S\_Q notation remain internal coordinate memory. This contact does not observe ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or a global biological law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the packet at bounded readout scope: a separate reality contact is required before any ribosome, protein-output, mechanism, structure, physical-admissibility, function, or universal-law claim; codon/window geometry cannot supply that contact.

This normalization is not a biological observation and does not convert BEDC codon/window geometry or tAI/stAI weights into translation realization, structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the residual-basis packet on the separated-contact route: use normalized ids for packet shape, then require a matched external dataset and Z-controlled statistic before any survival claim. Higher-layer claims still require their own reality contacts.

\subsection{Translation-conditioned four-case discriminant for $B^*_{Q6}$ residual coordinates}
\label{subsec:q6-translation-conditioned-four-case-survival-discriminant}

\paragraph{Finite row carrier.}
The record considered here is a finite matched table whose rows are coding-sequence entries and whose columns combine three kinds of coordinates: $B^*_{Q6}$ residual coordinates $q_i$, translation readouts $T_j$, and protein-omics readouts $P_k$.  The empirical table in run $d74f9f0e39cf$ joins $3739$ proteins for \emph{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655 and $5815$ proteins for \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}.  The translation readout is not ribosome profiling: it is the per-protein mean modeled tAI value
$$
\begin{aligned}
T &= \frac{\sum_{c} n_c\,w_c}{\sum_{c} n_c},
\end{aligned}
$$
where $n_c$ is the codon count in the coding sequence and $w_c$ is the codon weight obtained from GtRNAdb all-tRNA copy counts with dos Reis wobble weights.  The protein readout is $P=\log_{10}(\mathrm{abundance\_ppm})$ from measured PAXdb protein abundance joined to the same real coding-sequence codon counts.  The controls include amino-acid composition, organism-scoped sequence baselines, GC3, coding-sequence length, $M$-density, mRNA-related controls where present, and simpler codon-baseline controls.

\paragraph{Controlled matrices.}
For each organism, the calculation residualizes the $q_i$, $T_j$, and $P_k$ columns against the declared control matrix $Z$.  The discriminant then reads three controlled surfaces: $S^{QT}$ for residual coordinate survival against the translation readout, $S^{QP}$ for residual coordinate survival against the protein readout, and $M^{QTP}$ for the remaining $q_i \to P_k$ association after conditioning on $T_j$.  The classification threshold is $10^{-12}$, so the sign calls are not rounded narrative judgments; they are finite sign calls after the residualized matrix calculation.

\paragraph{Rank admissibility.}
The two organism panels are not the earlier rank-saturated three-row boundary.  In \emph{E. coli} K-12 MG1655 the control matrix has $24$ columns and rank $23$, leaving residual degrees of freedom $3716$.  The residualized $q$ block has rank $9$ against $T$ and rank $9$ against $P$, matching the $9$ available $q_i$ coordinates.  The residual energy of $T$ is $0.4728468725814029$, the residual energy of $P$ is $4020.333671802061$, the $q$-explained energy on $T$ is $0.256128714706293$, and the $q$-explained energy on $P$ is $432.89050065533684$.  In \emph{S. cerevisiae} the control matrix again has $24$ columns and rank $23$, leaving residual degrees of freedom $5792$.  The residualized $q$ block again has rank $9$ against both $T$ and $P$.  The residual energy of $T$ is $6.90537888244409$, the residual energy of $P$ is $2958.1865890925337$, the $q$-explained energy on $T$ is $5.671703404506426$, and the $q$-explained energy on $P$ is $882.6526210803958$.  Both panels therefore pass the powered-rank sufficiency check before any positive survival label is read.

\paragraph{Four cases.}
Each residual coordinate is assigned to one of four cases.  Case A is the double-null condition $S^{QT}\leq 0$ and $S^{QP}\leq 0$, where the controlled rows compress the coordinate away for both readouts.  Case B is $S^{QT}>0$ and $S^{QP}\leq 0$, marking a translation-level residual that does not propagate to the measured protein abundance readout.  Case C is $S^{QT}\leq 0$ and $S^{QP}>0$, marking a protein-omics prediction that is not mediated by the measured translation readout.  Case D is $S^{QT}>0$ and $S^{QP}>0$ together with reduction of the direct $q_i\to P_k$ association after conditioning on $T_j$, so the protein-abundance readout is classified as translation-conditioned propagation through the measured $T_j$ channel.  The controlled summary is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QT}(\mathrm{E.\ coli}) &= 0.5416736993691318, & R^2_{QP}(\mathrm{E.\ coli}) &= 0.10767526677985896,\\
R^2_{QT}(\mathrm{S.\ cerevisiae}) &= 0.8213457220900503, & R^2_{QP}(\mathrm{S.\ cerevisiae}) &= 0.29837624994140827.
\end{aligned}
$$
For \emph{E. coli}, the finite classification returns $A=0$, $B=0$, $C=0$, and $D=7$, with $2$ additional positive-positive coordinates that do not satisfy the mediation-drop requirement.  No coordinate is left unclassified.  For \emph{S. cerevisiae}, the finite classification returns $A=0$, $B=0$, $C=0$, and $D=9$, with $0$ positive-positive coordinates failing the mediation-drop requirement, and again no unclassified coordinate.

\paragraph{Readback.}
The result is a cross-layer discriminant, not a biological mechanism theorem.  It says that, after the named controls and rank checks, the measured finite rows support a four-case classification over modeled-tAI translation readouts and measured protein abundance.  In the two panels actually computed, the powered rows are dominated by case D: seven mediated coordinates in \emph{E. coli} and nine mediated coordinates in yeast, with the two \emph{E. coli} positive-positive non-drop entries held outside the mediated class.  The calculation may therefore be used to separate translation-only survival, protein-omics prediction not carried by the measured translation readout, and translation-conditioned protein-abundance propagation within this finite design.

\paragraph{Local boundary.}
No stronger biological claim follows from these rows.  The modeled tAI column is a bounded translation proxy, not an observed ribosome-occupancy measurement.  The PAXdb abundance column is a steady-state protein-abundance readout, not a folding assay, localization assay, stability assay, post-translational-state assay, molecular-function assay, physical-admissibility assay, perturbation experiment, phenotype measurement, or universal biological law.  A direct $q_i\to P_k$ association is not allowed to bypass the $q_i\to T_j$ conditioning step, and a case C call is not a translation mechanism.  Any claim about observed translation dynamics, folding, physical admissibility, function, mechanism, phenotype, or universality would require a separate layer-matched contact and its own controlled statistic.

\subsection{q6.rank-admissible.translation-survival-gate.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates across a rank-audited multi-organism panel with codon usage, tRNA gene-copy or tAI/stAI translation readouts, and controls for amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density

\paragraph{Current claim.} Before any B*\_Q6 coordinate is promoted from codon-topology residual to translation-realization survival, each proposed S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\} entry must pass a rank-admissibility gate: after residualizing q\_i and translation readout T\_j against Z, the remaining design must have non-saturated rank and enough independent organism/readout support to make held-out survival falsifiable. If the gate fails, the entry remains coupling-only or needs\_data even when the fitted in-sample score is positive or equal to 1.0.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite residualized design matrix over B*\_Q6 coordinates, organism-indexed translation readout surfaces, and control columns Z. Distinctions: rank-admissible translation-survival entry versus rank-saturated projection artifact; coupling-only contact versus controlled survival contact; translation-readout boundary before any protein, structure, function, or phenotype interpretation. Readback: per-entry admissibility certificate for S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\}: residualized rank, degrees of freedom after Z, held-out translation readout boundary, and break condition for rejecting survival when rank is saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-interaction-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through matched translation readouts and protein-abundance controls before any downstream protein-interaction or complex-membership readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-interaction or complex-membership readout only through a gated decomposition that first demonstrates rank-powered translation-readout survival and then tests whether conditioning on matched translation readout and protein abundance removes the direct q\_i association with the interaction readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite panel of coding sequences indexed by organism, B*\_Q6 residual coordinate, mandatory translation readout, protein-abundance control, and downstream protein-interaction readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient control or is compressed away; translation readout T\_j mediates or fails to mediate the q\_i to protein-interaction readout association; protein-interaction readout is interpreted only after translation-readout conditioning. Readback: per-entry gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-interaction readout with AA composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, mRNA, and protein-abundance controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout
