\subsection{Measured ribosome-efficiency survival for the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ residual basis}
\label{sec:q6-ribosome-te-survival-with-mrna-control-cross-layer}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
This record tests whether the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates remain visible at a measured ribosome-profiling translation-efficiency boundary after the amino-acid quotient and after explicit transcription separation.  The finite table has one row for each coding sequence that can be matched to the organism-specific codon table and to the local measured translation-efficiency file.  The response is $\log_{10}(\mathrm{TE})$, with $\mathrm{TE}=\mathrm{RPF}/\mathrm{mRNA}$ in the source rows, so the readout is already normalized by matched mRNA abundance before the regression layer is formed.  The residual coordinate block contains nine columns:
$$
\begin{aligned}
Q=\{&K\_AAA,\ Arg\_AGR,\ Ile\_AUA,\ Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR,\ Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG,\\
&Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY,\ Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG,\ Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY,\ f3\_stress\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
The control block $Z$ contains amino-acid composition, organism identity where applicable, $GC3$, CDS length, $M$-density, and the matched mRNA separation implicit in the measured TE construction.  A positive entry is admitted only when the $Q$ block retains nonzero explanatory power after these controls and when the design is not rank-saturated.

\paragraph{Joined measured rows.}
The measured-contact join is powered in both organisms recorded in the run.  For \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, $3739$ CDS rows were reported after codon joining, $3415$ genes had usable measured TE values, and the ribosome-TE join hit rate was $0.9133458143888741$.  For \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, $5815$ CDS rows were reported after codon joining, $5128$ genes had usable measured TE values, and the hit rate was $0.8818572656921754$.  These are not three-row or saturated panels: the residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ are $3392$ for \emph{E. coli} and $5105$ for yeast.

\paragraph{Rank and energy audit.}
The residualized control design has $24$ control columns in each organism and rank $23$ under tolerance $10^{-10}$.  The residualized $Q$ block has rank $9$ in both organisms, matching the nine declared residual coordinates.  The degeneracy cutoff for declaring an all-fit surface is $R^{2}=0.999999$, and neither organism is treated as degenerate.  In \emph{E. coli}, the explained energy from the $Q$ block after controls is $8.637891149939946$ against response residual energy $919.4854026798789$.  In yeast, the explained energy is $103.10404057290246$ against response residual energy $753.5781168800304$.  Thus the finite-row test is rank-admissible rather than a shape-only or underpowered contact.

\paragraph{Measured survival statistic.}
The run reports a positive controlled measured-TE statistic for both organisms.  For \emph{E. coli} K12 MG1655,
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^{2}_{QT,\mathrm{meas}} &= 0.009394266754822262,\\
\#\{\hbox{surviving coordinates}\} &= 9.
\end{aligned}
$$
For \emph{S. cerevisiae},
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^{2}_{QT,\mathrm{meas}} &= 0.13681931343730436,\\
\#\{\hbox{surviving coordinates}\} &= 9.
\end{aligned}
$$
The positivity threshold recorded for the partial-$R^{2}$ decision is $10^{-12}$.  Since both measured surfaces are positive, both have full residualized $Q$ rank, and both have thousands of residual degrees of freedom after controls, this finite contact supports survival of the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ residual block at the measured TE association layer for these two organism-specific panels.

\paragraph{Interpretation boundary.}
The supported assertion is deliberately narrow.  The statistic says that codon-residual coordinates improve a condition-specific measured TE readout after the declared controls.  It does not prove a universal law of translation efficiency, an elongation-speed mechanism, mature protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, or causal mediation.  Agreement or disagreement with modeled codon-adaptation weights is only coordinate-pattern evidence unless a separate measured contact supplies the relevant layer.  Likewise, the matched mRNA component separates the TE readout from transcript abundance; it is not by itself a translation output, protein output, structural measurement, functional assay, or mechanism assay.

\paragraph{Local verdict.}
For the claim $q6.ribosome\text{-}te\text{-}survival.with\text{-}mrna\text{-}control.cross\text{-}layer$, the finite rows are sufficient to mark the translation-efficiency association contact as ready.  The ready status attaches only to the measured $Q\to T$ surface described above: two organism-matched panels, nine residual coordinates, full residualized $Q$ rank, nondegenerate control residualization, and positive controlled $R^{2}_{QT,\mathrm{meas}}$ values.  Any promotion beyond this bounded measured-TE association requires an additional contact and an additional statistic at that layer.

\subsection{q6.three-matrix.translation-first.protein-readout.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates across coding sequences with matched codon usage, tAI or stAI translation weights, ribosome-profiling translation-efficiency readouts, mRNA abundance controls, and protein-abundance readouts

\paragraph{Current claim.} In a rank-powered matched panel, a B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-readout cross-layer claim only through the ordered decomposition S\textasciicircum{}QT first, then S\textasciicircum{}QP, then M\textasciicircum{}QTP; the conjecture is that at least one coordinate has positive translation-readout survival and that any downstream protein-abundance signal is partitionable into translation-mediated and non-mediated components under the stated controls.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite panel of coding sequences indexed by organism, B*\_Q6 residual coordinate, translation readout, and optional downstream protein-omics readout. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving synonymous residual coordinate versus amino-acid-sequence component; translation-readout survival entry S\textasciicircum{}QT\_\{ij\} versus rank-degenerate coupling entry A\textasciicircum{}QT\_\{ij\}; direct protein-omics survival S\textasciicircum{}QP\_\{ik\} versus translation-mediated component M\textasciicircum{}QTP\_\{ijk\}. Readback: rank-powered three-matrix readback boundary: S\textasciicircum{}QT must be estimated first under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls before any S\textasciicircum{}QP or M\textasciicircum{}QTP interpretation is promoted. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; rank.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{residual-basis.m-only-local-optimum-insufficiency}. If M-only is the true BEDC variable for DNA-to-protein, then for every organism and every readout in \{tAI, ribosome occupancy, protein abundance\}, adding any element of B*\_Q6 to an M-density baseline (with amino-acid composition controlled) does not increase the BEDC survival score. Equivalently, S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) <= 0 for every Q in B*\_Q6.

Support condition: Codon-usage tables for at least 3 organisms with tRNA-Leu, tRNA-Arg, tRNA-Ile, tRNA-Ser, tRNA-Thr copy-number data (e.g. Kazusa + GtRNAdb), plus a readout source (stAI or ribosome-profiling-derived efficiency).

Break condition: Some Q in B*\_Q6 has S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) > 0 in at least one organism on at least one readout; or CUN/UUR Leu log-ratio shows sign reversal between organisms after amino-acid composition control while Delta\_M does not.

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory matched translation readouts and protein-abundance mediation before any downstream functional-annotation readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as function-adjacent only if it first survives a rank-powered translation-readout test and then its downstream functional-annotation association is mediated through matched protein-abundance readouts under mRNA and baseline controls; otherwise the coordinate remains a translation/protein boundary object, not a function-realization claim.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory matched translation readouts before any protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may be proposed as a cross-layer localization-boundary candidate only if it first survives a rank-powered translation-readout test and then shows a bounded same-row relation to matched protein-localization readout under organism, amino-acid composition, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS-by-organism panel carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, same-row translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein-localization readback labels. Distinctions: AA-quotient-surviving codon residual coordinate q\_i versus amino-acid sequence baseline; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT(q\_i,T\_j|Z\_T) before any protein-localization interpretation; localization association only after conditioning on translation readout T\_j and mRNA controls. Readback: per-entry discriminant for q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-localization readout: compressed, translation-only, localization-predictive without measured translation mediation, or translation-mediated localization boundary. Internal structure: coordinate; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\paragraph{Mismatch ledger.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion.scope-review}. Status: aligned. Delta: The tAI or stAI contact supplies an external organism-level codon-adaptation weight readback for the residual-basis packet, while BEDC residual coordinates and S\_Q notation remain internal coordinate memory. This contact does not observe ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or a global biological law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the packet at bounded readout scope: a separate reality contact is required before any ribosome, protein-output, mechanism, structure, physical-admissibility, function, or universal-law claim; codon/window geometry cannot supply that contact.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights.blocked-shape.normalized-id-boundary}. Status: blocked\_null. Delta: The canonical ids are codon-usage-per-organism, ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency, and orf-eligibility.seed.boundary. This normalization is not a biological observation and does not convert BEDC codon/window geometry or tAI/stAI weights into translation realization, structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law.

Refinement pressure: Keep the residual-basis packet on the separated-contact route: use normalized ids for packet shape, then require a matched external dataset and Z-controlled statistic before any survival claim. Higher-layer claims still require their own reality contacts.

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.mrna-control-id-shape}. Status: blocked\_null. Delta: The residual-basis no-promotion packet remains scoped to separated contacts: tAI or stAI weights can be bounded codon-adaptation readbacks, while matched mRNA abundance is only a control covariate. Neither the invalid token nor the normalized contact id converts BEDC residual coordinates, S\_Q notation, codon/window geometry, or transcript counts into ribosome occupancy, protein output, structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-localization-readout.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through same-row matched translation readouts before any matched protein-localization readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched CDS panel, each B*\_Q6 coordinate may be tested against protein-localization readouts only after the mandatory translation-readout boundary is attached; admissible outcomes distinguish compression, translation-only survival, localization association not mediated by measured translation, and translation-conditioned localization mediation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-protein-localization-readout}. per-protein localization, compartment, fractionation, imaging, or proximity-labeling readouts for externally measured or curated proteins

Can test: protein-localization readout after organism and protein/CDS identifier matching; cross\_layer\_relation boundary only after a separate same-row translation readout and matched controls are attached. Cannot test: translation realization from localization rows alone; protein folding; structural order; physical admissibility; function realization; system phenotype; protein trafficking mechanism; compartment identity mechanism; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite same-row CDS surface carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, a matched translation readout, mRNA controls, and a matched protein-localization label/readout. Distinctions: q\_i is first residualized against amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls; translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\} must be non-vacuous before localization readback is interpreted; localization readout is treated only as a bounded protein-state surface, not as function or phenotype. Readback: per-entry relation q\_i -> T\_j -> L\_k where T\_j is a translation readout and L\_k is a matched protein-localization readout, with null status if the translation step is absent or rank-degenerate. Internal structure: relation; closure; rank.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{q6.three-matrix.protein-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. A three-matrix Q6 packet may read matched proteomics only as a bounded protein-abundance surface after the S\textasciicircum{}QT translation-readout step; any PTM, stability, localization, folding, physical-admissibility, function, phenotype, or global-law claim requires a separate layer-matched reality contact.

Support condition: The report keeps B*\_Q6 residual geometry, rank-powered S\textasciicircum{}QT/S\textasciicircum{}QP/M\textasciicircum{}QTP notation, modeled or measured translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and matched proteomics abundance as distinct contacts and statistics.

Break condition: The packet treats protein-abundance association, translation-conditioned mediation language, codon/window geometry, modeled tAI or stAI weights, or gate-pass status as evidence for PTM, stability, localization, folding, structure, physical admissibility, biological function, phenotype, mechanism, or universal biological law.

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout
